# Traffic and Dragons — TODO archive

Completed rows moved out of [TODO.md](../TODO.md) on 2026-09-01, byte-identical, section by section. Row numbers are global across both files: `grep -n "^| 173 |" TODO.md DOC/TODO_ARCHIVE.md` finds a row wherever it lives. This file is append-only — a row that closes moves here in its own commit (the tracker lint verifies moved rows by number). Full records for the largest rows live in [DOC/todos_completed/](todos_completed/).

Noise, not damage: nothing was withheld that mattered, but six engine notes taught the GM a ceremony for a corpse that needed none. **Design question (Fable):** the encounter's slain foes vanish with the encounter, so the validator has nothing to match against; keeping a bounded `worldState.combatSlain` ring (name + turn, cap 12, retired by the same summary that cites it) would let a summary death whose subject was slain in the combat record validate as combat-canon instead of opening a conflict. Drift surface (W2 validator + a new worldState field). | S | Fable | ○ Filed 2026-09-01 from audits/AUDIT_playtest_v1767_sonnet5.md. |

| 296 | **Blueprint designer: Save never blocks, completeness is a PUBLISH gate (owner call 2026-09-01).** TLDR: Save and Publish shared one validation gate, so an author who left an act without arcs could not save a file and finish the blueprint in a later sitting — the one-slot per-browser localStorage draft was the only way to pause. **Mechanism:** `designerValidate()` ran identically before the Save download and the Publish upsert. **Why the split is safe:** a saved FILE is re-validated by every consumer that opens it (the wizard import refuses with the same message, the designer's Load reopens it with the issue named), while the cloud library feeds `startGame` WITHOUT re-validating (game.js E19) — so Publish is the one gate that must stay. **Shipped:** Save always downloads; the status line names unreadiness in amber ("Saved x.blueprint — not publish-ready: Act 1 has no arcs.") or says "publish-ready" in green — never silent; Publish unchanged. Designer contract clause ⑧ (run-tests.js) pins all three facts, sabotage-proven 3/3 (save gate restored / publish gate removed / unreadiness silenced each fail the suite). Verified live through `__bpdTest`: incomplete → download + amber note, publish refused before any library write; completed → green + published. | S | — | ✅ **SHIPPED designer v0.39** (2026-09-01; engine untouched, APP_VERSION unchanged per the designer carve-out). |

| 293 | **Queue-mode rendering — fal's queue API for slow engines + real progress (owner diagnosis 2026-08-31: "the problem isn't the slowness, the problem is that it wasn't apparent anything was happening").** TLDR: the render proxy is synchronous (fal.run), so any engine slower than Fly's ~60s proxy idle cutoff can NEVER deliver — the 55s timeout makes that failure loud, but it permanently locks out slow engines (the Seedream 4-ref party edit ran >120s). fal's queue API (queue.fal.run: submit → request_id → poll status → fetch result) fixes both halves: no long-held connection (each poll is a fast request, immune to the idle cutoff) and REAL progress (queue position / in-progress status → the client status line shows actual state, not a hope). Build: server routes `/api/render-queue/submit|status|result` (same allowlist/entitlement/metering; meter on submit), client falFetch grows a queued variant used when the engine declares `slow:true` in RENDER_MODELS (table data, per the idiom), status line shows queue state + the v1.756 elapsed ticker. The 55s sync path stays for fast engines (nano ~14s) — no queue latency tax on the common case. | M | Any | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.757** (2026-08-31, same session as filing — owner green-light "throw that in now"). Server: `/api/render-queue/submit/*` (same allowlist/entitlement/cap, metered at submit) + `/api/render-queue/poll` (one status/result GET, HOST-PINNED to the queue base — the SSRF door stays shut); battery 54. Client: `falQueueRender` (api.js — server or BYOK route, 5-min give-up, status callback), Seedream's entry carries `slow:true`, doRender branches on it and the v1.756 ticker's base text now shows REAL state ("queued (#2)" / "rendering") with elapsed seconds; transport battery 37. Portrait paths deliberately stay sync (solo t2i fits the window). |
| 286 | **W2 same-scene overflow latch is unclearable by the summary its own refusal copy prescribes (joint review f59, verified against current identity.js 2026-08-29).** TLDR: when a big-cast scene overflows `SCENE_REF_ACTOR_CAP`=10 (or the negatives cap) WITHOUT a node change, `_sceneRefOverflow` stamps the latch with the ACTIVE scene serial, and `sceneRefsSummarySuccess` clears overflow only when `overflow.scene!==active.scene` — so every death in that scene fails closed (`w2DeathAuthorized` returns false even for fully-evidenced bound actors; `sceneRefBind`/`sceneRefExclude` also refuse, so the GM cannot even record the evidence to heal), while the refusal reason AND the prompt block both prescribe a structured summary that CANNOT clear it. The capacity-path refusal also suppresses `_w2ArmDeathValve`, so the `[NPC_DEATH_REPORTED:]` fork note never fires — the misleading summary prescription is the only guidance the GM gets. Real exits today, taught nowhere: any NODE change (incl. entering/leaving a sublocation — verifier) followed by a summary, or `[NPC_DEATH_REPORTED:]`. Verified: entry-13's bounded sealed-frame buffer did NOT touch this — it covers transitions UNDER a latch, not the same-scene latch itself; the pinned test covers only transitioned-overflow recovery. **Design fork needing an owner ruling (the fail-closed breadth was deliberate):** (a) a summary success under a same-scene latch honestly recovers capacity (clear + re-latch on the next over-cap event — the argument `sceneRefsSummarySuccess` already accepts for sealed frames); (b) narrow the latch so already-BOUND actors' deaths stay authorized and only new registrations refuse; (c) minimum: the same-scene refusal copy names the real exits. Missing test either way: summary while the latched scene is still active. **RULED 2026-08-29 (owner, via chip): option (a) + (c) — a successful structured summary under a same-scene latch clears the overflow (re-latching allowed on the next over-cap event), AND the refusal copy/prompt block name the real exits.** | M | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.749** (2026-08-29) — `sceneRefsSummarySuccess` clears overflow UNCONDITIONALLY (ordering keeps fail-closed honest: the clear runs at the END of `applySummaryExtract`, after `w2ValidateSummary`, so the clearing summary's own death claims still validate UNDER the latch — the #168R7 order pin stays green); the refusal reason (all 3 sites incl. the #213 registry row, census green) and the EVIDENCE CAPACITY WARNING prompt line now teach all three exits (summary / scene move incl. sublocation / `[NPC_DEATH_REPORTED:]`); the capacity path still deliberately never arms the death valve. 2 failing-first engine tests (same-scene clear + re-latch + bound-death-after-recovery; copy exits at both surfaces), suite 1851 green; 3 new sabotage clauses in dev/sabotage-w2.js, 3/3 proven at authoring, byte-identical restore. |

| 253 | **THE PAGE-HIDE FLUSH IS SIZE-BOUNDED — and the turns it can't flush are pushed at next launch (JP0-11, joint review 2026-08-27; Fable f68, verified ×2).** TLDR: closing or backgrounding the game was documented as never dropping the final turn — and that guarantee was dead for almost every real save, silently. Mechanism: the unload/page-hide flush rides `fetch(keepalive:true)`; the Fetch spec caps total in-flight keepalive request BODIES at 64 KiB, browsers reject anything larger, and the rejection landed in a `.catch(function(){})` — no console line, no badge, no toast, `_onSyncFail` never called. Not a mature-campaign edge case either: the PC's base64 portrait rides **inline** by design (`_stripNpcPortraits` strips only NPC avatars), so a 40–100KB data URL clears the cap from roughly turn 1. The loss is cross-device: the unflushed turns stay local, and a second device that then advances PAST them makes this device's next reconcile adopt the server copy wholesale (if it does NOT advance past them, the same silent flush failure instead surfaces as the loud CAS 409 — a different, visible face of one defect). Shipped: over `FLUSH_KEEPALIVE_MAX` (56KB, measured in BYTES — `payload.length` is a character count and under-reports the multi-byte `{__lz}` transcript) the doomed request is **not attempted**; the flush marks the campaign in `tnd_sync_dirty_v1` (`{campId: turn}`, `store` wrapper, cap 8) and says so in the console. The next `load()` **pushes before `_reconcileFromServer` may adopt**, deferring — never blocking — the reconcile; the CAS turn guard is untouched, so a genuine two-device conflict still pauses loudly. The marker clears ONLY on a confirmed 2xx of our own payload at a turn ≥ the marked one; one toast per page load. CLAUDE.md §16's false guarantee line was corrected in the same commit. | S | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.720** (2026-08-28) — 14 failing-first assertions (5 synchronous in the suite + 9 in the new standalone `dev/tests-jp011-flush-dirty.js`, which drives `load()`'s wholesale global rewrite); mutation proof `dev/sabotage-jp011-flush-dirty.js` **10/10**, byte-identical restore. Field watch: the boot toast is the visible proof the marker round-trips on a real device. |

| 231 | **THE ARC WALL — an emergent thread dies with the arc that bore it (owner observation + HARD-WALL ruling 2026-08-24, after finishing the first blueprint campaign).** TLDR: the owner finished Runelords and reported emergent quests as wildly uneven — “the candy heist was a lot of fun” vs “The Bells, and the closed eye, ‘Spire’ just dragged on and on” — ending with **“by the end of that campaign I just wanted it over, and that is the OPPOSITE of how a player should feel near the end”** (the north star: that feeling is a measurable failure, not a mood). **Two intuitive explanations were MEASURED AND FALSIFIED against the t2324 save:** duration is not the problem (Sugar War ran 161 turns from first mention and was the favourite), and epistemic-vs-terminal goals is not the problem (zero-box rates 3/13 vs 3/11; mystery quests like The Scarred Man 4/4 and The Glassworks 3/3 were fine). **Verifiability matters but is NOT sufficient — owner's decisive correction: “even if the closed eye line were verifiable, 18 threads is WAY too long… the closed eye dominated the main quest which can't be allowed to happen.”** **The real violation is PROPORTION:** authored spine = 3 acts / 10 arcs; the closed-eye thread = **18 quests, 1.8× the entire spine**, spanning **1,458 of 2,324 turns (63% of the campaign)**; 90% of all 51 quests were emergent. Mechanism: it began legitimately inside Act 1's “The Skinsaw Man” arc and then **outlived its parent by ~1000 turns across ~8 arc boundaries** with nothing ever asking whether it should still exist — while the giants/Jorgenfist thread (7 quests, 262 turns) was FINE precisely because it died with its arc. #191's stale-quest nudge is structurally blind to this: it fires on SILENCE (30 turns, no tag activity) and a hydra thread is loud — tags firing, quests closing on schedule, every indicator green. **Shipped fix — the arc wall:** an emergent quest is stamped `bornArc` at creation (`questIsEmergent` + `currentArcTitle`, helpers.js — one source for stamp and sweep so they can never disagree about what “spine” means); when `[ARC_COMPLETE:]` fires, live progeny of that arc are archived **`abandoned`** (the #229 status — the reopen guard blocks force-reactivation, a fresh `\|offered` may still bring it back if the story genuinely re-raises it), loudly (muts + toast + system message + console). `buildArcWallNudge` (NOTE_BUILDERS, combat-silent, per-arc cooldown) gives `ARC_WALL_WARN_LEAD`=15 turns of RUNWAY — naming the arc and every doomed thread so the GM can land them in fiction — but the wall falls either way: **no promotion path, by explicit owner ruling.** A thread that deserves campaign scale must be promoted to the SPINE (become an arc), which is the honest outcome — **and promotion is an AUTHORING-TIME act (f13 clarifier, 2026-08-29): it means editing the blueprint for the NEXT campaign; no tag, tool, or UI adds an arc to a running skeleton, by design under this ruling.** **Deliberately NARROW, three fail-safes:** spine-titled quests are never stamped (an arc can't sweep its own quest); UNSTAMPED quests are permanently immune (legacy saves — verified inert on the live t2324 save, both quests immune, `[ARC_COMPLETE:]` sweeps nothing); a PARALLEL act stamps nothing (an ambiguous parent would sweep innocents). | M | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.714** (2026-08-24) — 7 failing-first engine tests (suite 1678 green, latch registry + note-builder contracts re-censused clean); both fixed with real coverage. Field watch: the wall only binds quests created from v1.714 on — the first new campaign is the real test. |

| 228 | **The split re-affirm LOOP — the engine asked, the GM answered, and the answer wiped the question (field t2320-t2324, owner-ruled 2026-08-24 ⓒ).** TLDR: the player said “you three stay here, I'll scout ahead”; five turns later the save showed all three companions re-splitting to the party's own node EVERY turn, the guestbook had five phantom “arrivals” at a place nobody moved to, and the SPLIT AUDIT note was firing 12 turns out of 12. The trigger was a missing `[SUBLOCATION:]` — Ammut walked throne room → archway → stair → bone-ring chamber with nothing filed, so the split target and the party's node stayed identical. | 205 | **Every quest carries its END CONDITION as a checkbox — the t2101 "Pinnacle of Avarice" false completion (owner-ruled 2026-08-21).** TLDR: the final-act quest completed off a bracelet errand in a Magnimar bathhouse scene because its actual goal (confront the Runelord atop Xin-Shalast) lived only in the desc — the objective list was 1/1 done, so the #20 all-objectives-complete channels truthfully told the GM to close it. | 218 | **Sabotage newline rot — multi-line find strings could never match CRLF files (harness class, found 2026-08-22 while proving #213).** TLDR: `sabotage.prove` matched clause `find` strings by exact `indexOf`; every source file is CRLF on disk, so any clause whose find contained a bare LF escape was structurally unmatchable — it printed `NOT APPLIED` and proved nothing, forever. 39 clauses across 14 sabotage files were candidates; 5 confirmed dead in live runs, two of them drift-surface guards (`combat-close propagation bypasses the scene ledger` in sabotage-w2 — the B3 kill-stamp bypass; `band distance degrades to always-far` in sabotage-phase — the #158 in-band silencer). Truly-absent finds still hard-fail NOT APPLIED (pinned). **Proof:** 2 failing-first meta-tests in `tests-sabotage-meta.js` (suite 5→7; rides CI via run-standalone-suites) + a clause in `sabotage-standalone-suites.js` that deletes the normalizer and demands the meta suite red (8/8; `dev/sabotage.js` joins its synthetic copy set; the scratch-isolation meta test skips LOUDLY in the no-git synthetic tree, still covered by the real-repo pass). **Wake-up audit:** sabotage-w2 + sabotage-phase re-run under the fixed harness (results in row status); the other 12 candidate files re-run as a follow-up batch — any newly-woken clause verdicting MISSED is a guard that never worked and gets fixed as its own finding. | S | Any (dev tooling) | ✅ Harness fix + meta proof + standalone clause SHIPPED v1.701 (2026-08-22, on Fable). | 217 | **Schedule near-duplicate dedupe — the triple-booked bracelet deadline (field, live t2097, 2026-08-22).** TLDR: the live clock carried THREE deadlines for one errand — "Return to Wyla Ashvane's shop to collect the four cold-warded bracelets" (7.4d) / "Collect the completed ember-rune warding bracelets" (6.8d) / "…finish tempering … in three days" (2.9d) — all injected into UPCOMING every turn, and the contradiction reached the fiction (Wyla arguing six days at t2087 while an entry said three). **Root cause:** `scheduleAdd` deduped by EXACT lowercase label only; its sibling store `fileFutureEvent` got stemmed-fingerprint near-dup dedupe as #29① for precisely this re-phrasing failure, and the tooth never landed on the clock schedule — even though #150 extracted `feNearDup` specifically so schedule code could share thresholds. **Shipped, both halves through the ONE shared fingerprint (no rival heuristic):** ① write-time — `scheduleAdd` folds a near-dup label into the existing entry, keeping the original label and adopting the NEWEST deadline (last-write-wins, exactly the exact-match semantics; the GM's latest statement of a deal is the freshest information); ② load-time — `scheduleDedupSweep` (clock.js, called from `migrateWorldState`) collapses twins an existing save already carries: born-DESC greedy grouping so the freshest-born entry wins by construction, complete pre-images archived to `clock.repairs`, idempotent, never touches `clock.min` (the #146 never-auto-heal rule guards the scalar/anchors; this removes redundant rows, reversibly). **Verified against the REAL save, not a synthetic twin:** replaying the actual t2097 export collapses 3→1 keeping sch3 — the three-day deal the fiction was actually arguing about — with both stale phrasings archived and re-run a no-op; the shipped fingerprint separates the control pair ("Meet Wyla at the shop" / "Ambush the caravan"). Judgment call recorded: last-write-wins on write + freshest-born on sweep were chosen without an owner ruling (the owner greenlit the build without answering the merge-policy question) — both match existing exact-match/fileFutureEvent semantics and the live fiction; re-rule if the field disagrees. |
| 216 | **[TIME_CHECK:] — the GM reads the clock before it writes (owner design 2026-08-22, the t2175 sundown-at-midday).** TLDR: sonnet-5 narrated nightfall and camped the party in full dark while the clock read 11:57 AM, and #158's prose recognizer — precision-tuned on the 328-turn audit — was structurally blind to it (nightfall rendered as pure imagery: "day bleeding out of the sky"; `clockPhaseAssertion` returned null on both failing turns and only fired on the correction). Widening the recognizer's grammar would trade in the false alarms that audit exists to prevent, so the fix is UPSTREAM of recognition: the owner's read-then-write proposal, made structural. **Shipped:** the GM opens every response with `[TIME_CHECK:phase]` — the #141 forced-checking-space shape (committing to a phase before writing prose anchors the prose) — and detection becomes a deterministic BAND COMPARISON instead of prose recognition: `clockCheckDeclared` (clock.js) resolves the label via the one `TIME_PHASES` vocabulary, compares via `clockPhaseBandDist`, and an off-band declaration ≥`PHASE_MISMATCH_MIN` arms the SAME #158 one-shot `phaseMismatch` → `buildPhaseMismatchNudge` (one latch, one nudge, no #151 census change). **Read-only by construction, and it is the design's load-bearing wall:** TIME_CHECK never moves the clock — `[TIME:]` reconciles and `[TIME_ADVANCE:]` charges; a declaration that could advance time would let the GM teleport the clock by assertion, the exact back door #73 closed. Table position BEFORE the TIME family = the declaration is judged against the pre-advance clock the GM actually read. Unmappable labels warn loudly and arm nothing. Doc line joins the clock family in the STABLE half (deliberate frozen-hash re-baseline, +582 chars doc / +11 strip; one cache re-warm on next live turn). Against the field failure: `[TIME_CHECK:sundown]` at 11:57 AM arms the reconcile nudge on the SAME turn instead of two turns of wrong-phase narration and a player question. | S | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.700** (2026-08-22, built ON Fable — drift review recorded here per the amended decree). 6 engine tests (2 defining reds first; 4 guard pins proven by mutation) + 3 sabotage clauses (teleportation write / dead arm / dead gate — all caught); diff-replay on 2 corpora: zero handler errors, historical responses unaffected. **Field watch (the #207 pattern):** compliance is free to measure — `tagLog` records tag names per response; judge emission rate + whether anchoring reduces phase drift over the next live sessions. Escalation if under-emitted: a one-shot missing-check note (the #20 channel lesson), not a stronger doc line. Deliberately NOT in TAG_REINFORCE (the clock family isn't there today, and the failing model is Claude, which never sees reinforce). |
| 214 | **The narration killed it, the tracker did not — the Grey-Hided Skulker desync (field, owner-reported 2026-08-22).** TLDR: the prose closed a fight ("the last of the grey things finally goes down") while Skulker C stood at 8/32 in the combat panel, because the response emitted no combat tag at all — not the killing `[ENEMY_HP:]`, not `[ENEMY_SLAIN:]`, not `[COMBAT_END:]`. **Owner ruling:** the GM saying "sorry, it's still alive" is a worse outcome than reconciling — remove the remaining HP and call it dead. Best case is a GM that follows the rules, but that cannot be the only lever (#60b: a prohibition without a channel does not change behavior; the gpt-4o finding: tag discipline is a separable capability). **The design boundary that makes this safe, and it is the whole design: prose wins for `combat.foes[]`, evidence still wins for roster NPCs.** W2 fails closed on identity because a wrong corpse is unrecoverable canon; a rolled foe has no identity stakes and its HP exists to serve the narration. Both levers are deterministic — **no prose scan anywhere** (the instrument #175b rejected as non-deterministic). **① `[COMBAT_END:` + a victory-shaped outcome word resolves the survivors** (tag_table.js). Previously a victory close with living foes nulled the tracker and discarded them silently — never marked slain, never propagated. They are now zeroed and marked slain *before* `propagateSlainFoes`, so a **rostered** foe still routes through the same scene-evidence gate as any other named death (pinned by a test: an unwitnessed rostered foe is NOT killed by the outcome word). fled/truce/disengaged/defeat resolve nothing — those mean the foe stopped fighting, not that it died. **② `buildCombatStaleNudge` asks when an open encounter goes `COMBAT_STALE_TURNS`=2 turns with no combat tag** (api.js, first in NOTE_BUILDERS, latch `combatStalePing`). It measures SILENCE — engine-observed tag activity stamped at the post-handler seam — never prose. Deliberately NOT combat-silent like its siblings: it is the one note whose entire subject is an open fight. | 215 | **A withheld reward becomes a decision, not a loss — the claim modal (owner ruling 2026-08-22, amends #213).** TLDR: #213 made the shelve notice honest ("the withheld reward will not arrive — award it by hand with the Sync button"), and the owner ruled the fix wrong in its last step: **"going into Sync isn't an option — Sync is too much of an immersion breaker."** Open a modal and ask the player instead. | 128 | **NPC identity fragmentation — one person, many memory entries.** TLDR: the same character accumulates separate memory entries under name variants — the live Runelords save has 62 memory entries for 37 actual NPCs, with Sheriff Hemlock existing four times ("Sheriff Belor Hemlock" / "Sheriff Hemlock" / "Belor Hemlock" / "Hemlock"), so his history is split four ways: recall pulls from whichever fragment a scene happens to match, and every fragment looks thinner than the person really is. Recorded so it isn't re-litigated at migration time; the near-term variant scan is required under either storage scheme (the pointer table records "same person" cheaply but can't DECIDE it). ⚠ Drift surface (memory tiers + alias resolution) — Fable tier, design review before code. | M | Fable | ✅ **COMPLETE (v1.528, 2026-08-03; closed at the 2026-08-20 split)** — the deterministic variant scan was this row's deliverable: (2026-08-03): `npcVariantTokens`/`npcVariantPairs`/`scanNpcNameVariants` (memory.js) run every summarize, feeding the existing #57 queue via the extracted shared `_queueMergeHint` (once-ever latch + dedupe, one implementation for both producers). Deliberately does NOT reuse `npcCoreTokens` — that stripper drops role nouns (man/woman/stranger), safe only under resolve-time's exactly-one-candidate guard; at proposal time those words are identity-bearing. Ambiguity rules: a bare surname matching two different fuller names proposes nothing (the Perdrath-sisters case); a party member is always the canonical side. | 161 | **"⟳ Update from library" on character sheets — the pull half of the character-editor loop (user request 2026-08-10).** TLDR: the player sheet and party companion sheets get an Update-from-library button that pulls the library copy's IDENTITY fields into the live character behind a per-field old→new preview — progression is never touched, because the library copy is a SNAPSHOT (Daeris can sit there at Lv3 while she's Lv9 in play) and a full replace would wipe campaign progress. Editor-era follow-ups recorded here: per-field checkboxes on the preview; voiceId/languages as registry candidates. |
| 159 | **TTS: interword hyphens read as pauses — "half-buried" spoke as "half … buried" (field report 2026-08-10).** TLDR: hyphenated compounds (half-buried, cargo-culting) paused mid-word on the Piper-class tiers; the hyphen now becomes a space before the engine ever sees it, so compounds read as adjacent words while the comma/period rhythm the user likes stays untouched. **Mechanism (grounded on the t1593 corpus):** the GM writes compounds with plain ASCII hyphens — 1011 instances across 1.8MB of prose, in 636 of 1592 GM entries (40% of all narration), and ZERO em/en dashes anywhere — but `normalizeForTTS` only ever handled `--` and em/en dashes, and the unit splitter never breaks at hyphens, so the compound reached Piper intact and espeak-ng phonemized the hyphen as an audible pause INSIDE the synthesized wav — the one place the scheduled inter-unit gaps (comma 0.15s / full stop 0.44s) can't reach. **Fix (tts.js):** one lookahead rule in `normalizeForTTS` — `([A-Za-z])-(?=[A-Za-z])` → letter+space; the unconsumed trailing letter means chained compounds ("three-and-a-half") strip every link. Scope deliberately letters-only: digit ranges ("3-4"), spaced breaks (" - "), and the `--`/em-dash dashRepl rules untouched; unconditional across engines (OS voices speak both forms identically, so native is neutral and the paths can't drift). | S | Any | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.585** (2026-08-10). 2 failing-first tests + 2 pins (scope boundaries; B14 unit-count invariance — persisted speaker maps key on counts) → 1199 ALL GREEN; real-data check through the shipped pipeline: 5 real hyphen-bearing t1593 entries → 232 units, zero surviving interword hyphens, zero unit-count changes. piper_test.html's spike-era normalize copy left as-is (historical, pre-harvest, already drifted). |

Red-first: due audit + dead provider → the audit fires again next turn; successful turn → normal cooldown; TT/silent paths untouched. <br><br>**Evidence:** Sol R6, Fable-verified (buildRelationshipAudit stamps at compose time; game.js builds notes before callGM; the catch restores nothing) — a due split-audit probe burned its stamp with no delivery, and t1547's ignored split audit consumed its cooldown anyway. | 150 | **DRIFT HARDENING: one future, one lifecycle — schedule promotion retires the futureEvents twin; every expiry archives; quest-linked threads get one question.** TLDR: anticipated events live in two stores with different lifecycles; promotion retires the twin, every expiry archives, quest-linked threads get one GM question first. <br><br>**Solution:** ① `scheduleAdd` (clock.js) cross-checks pending `memory.futureEvents` with the EXISTING fileFutureEvent near-dup fingerprint (feTokens ≥2 shared + half-fingerprint — reuse, no new heuristic); a match retires that event to `memory.archive.futureEvents` stamped `promoted:<scheduleId>` (loud console.info) — one lifecycle authority, Sol's linkage shape. ② `expireFutureEvents` (memory.js) archives EVERY expiry to `memory.archive.futureEvents` (today they vanish — the void class again); an expiring event whose fingerprint matches an ACTIVE quest title or objective instead arms ONE one-shot GM note ("this aged-out thread looks quest-linked — resolve it, re-file it with [FUTURE_EVENT:], or stay silent and it dies") and then expires-with-archive at the next sweep regardless — bounded, and NO setTurn rewrite ever (Sol's immortal-event objection honored; Fable's original token-overlap refresh is withdrawn). | 149 | **DRIFT HARDENING: durable world changes get filed — the stateNotes sink stops evicting, then a node-anchored post-combat nudge feeds it.** TLDR: [LOCATION_STATE:] is built but starving — zero emissions in 40 responses; harden the cap's eviction to archive, then nudge after combat at the exact changed node. <br><br>**Solution:** sink first (Sol's ordering): ① `fileLocationState` eviction → `memory.archive.locationStates` (the prompt promises a durable change record; eviction must be recoverable), and a one-time migrate sweep drops literal junk notes ("none"/empty — Sandpoint carries one, logged). ② Emission: combat close (the `[COMBAT_END:]` handler AND the all-foes-down auto-close) stamps `worldState.pendingLocState={node: currentNodeKey(), turn}`; new NOTE_BUILDERS entry `buildLocationStateNudge` fires next turn ONLY while `currentNodeKey()` still equals the stamped node ("if that fight left durable marks on this place, file [LOCATION_STATE:]; if nothing durable changed, stay silent") — party departed → dropped silently, never filed onto the wrong node (Sol's mis-anchor hazard); the pending object clears in `commitGmTurn`, not at build, so a failed turn keeps the shot (the #151 discipline, local). ③ Telemetry: tagLog already records both sides — watch nudge-fires vs LOCATION_STATE emissions; the settle-nudge extension and the verb-watcher stay shelved unless emission stays near zero. Red-first: fires at the node, drops on departure, survives a failed turn, eviction archives, junk-note sweep. <br><br>**Evidence:** Fable Finding 3 (tagLog 0/40; 3 stateNotes nodes lifetime — and Sol found one is literally "none", so 2 real across 77 nodes / 3,096 transcript entries) + the prompt-channel-beats-position class proven twice on this project: doc-only tags starve (SAY was 0 until nudged, now 39/40; LOCATION_DESC 8% until #134). | S | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.568** (2026-08-08, drift pass order 5). Emission: `worldState.combat.node` recorded at COMBAT_START, and BOTH close paths (explicit `[COMBAT_END:]` + all-foes-down auto-close) stamp `worldState.pendingLocState` at the fight's OWN node — a close+move response can't mis-anchor (red-first-proven: the Rat/Greyford shape stamps Ashfen), and a same-response `[LOCATION_STATE:]` suppresses the stamp. `buildLocationStateNudge` (NOTE_BUILDERS, beside its sibling buildLocationDescNudge): fires next turn ONLY while the party is still at the node; departure clears silently (Sol's mis-anchor hazard); holds without consuming during a new fight; **consumed at COMMIT, not build** — a failed provider call keeps the shot (the #151 discipline, local). 5 red-first tests → green (1104). 🧪 Field watch: tagLog now records nudge-yield — if LOCATION_STATE emission stays ~0 after real combats, the settle-nudge extension comes off the shelf. |

| 147 | **DRIFT HARDENING: corrections survive their own retcon — pin the corrected truth until memory files it.** TLDR: [RETCON:] de-indexes the correction along with the mistake; a bounded pin keeps the corrected fact injected until the next summarize lands, then archives. <br><br>**Solution:** the `[RETCON:]` observation site (logTranscript's rc-marking, state.js) also sets `worldState.retconPin = {what: <the tag's own payload text>, turn}` — the payload already states what was corrected, no prose excerpting. `buildSysPrompt` (volatile half) injects one line while the pin lives: "CORRECTION IN FORCE (tN): <what> — the corrected telling is canon; the earlier one is void." Cleared by the FIRST of: a successful `summarize()` completion (the extractor saw the corrected exchange while it was still in sessionLog) or `RETCON_PIN_SHELF`=15 turns; on clear the pin archives to `memory.archive.retconPins` — never a silent drop, and shelf expiry also console.warns. Single pin slot: a newer retcon archives the prior. ""-clean when absent; stable half untouched (byte-identity suite). Red-first: pin injects; summarize clears+archives; shelf expiry clears loudly; a second retcon rotates the slot. <br><br>**Evidence (audit round):** `logTranscript` marks BOTH the preceding GM entry and the correcting GM entry `rc=1`; `_ragRetrieveScore` excludes every `rc` entry, and its separate `GM:` meta filter would also exclude the correcting response. That is safe against replaying the false version, but it leaves the corrected truth dependent on the rolling session tail and a later summarizer extraction. Latest-save proof (t1547→t1548): the false street interrogation and the corrected exchange with Wyla are both de-indexed; Wyla's current knowledge does not yet contain the corrected fact that the two watchers are hers. An exact A/B probe returns no RAG excerpt with both entries marked, and returns the correction when the correcting entry is allowed. Counterweight: the correction is still in the current sessionLog and may be captured by the next summary, so this is a delayed/probabilistic canon hole, not evidence that the latest turn has already forgotten it. Candidate direction: first-class correction records — superseded entry excluded, corrected replacement explicitly canonical and eligible (bypassing the `GM:` meta filter), or pin the correction until memory filing acknowledges it. | S | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.567** (2026-08-08, drift pass order 4). Exactly per the solution: the rc-marking site (logTranscript) also pins the tag's own payload to `worldState.retconPin` (single slot, a newer retcon rotates the prior into `memory.archive.retconPins`); `buildRetconPinBlock` (api.js, volatile, ""-clean) injects "CORRECTION IN FORCE (tN): …" while the pin lives; cleared by the FIRST of a completed `applySummaryExtract` (runs after the chapter file, so a retried window keeps the pin) or `RETCON_PIN_SHELF`=15 turns — every exit archives, shelf expiry console.warns. Archive key wired through memArchive/healMemory/the import whitelist/the #144A contract keys. 5 red-first tests → green (1099); stable-half byte identity suite-held. Origin: Sol R5 (t1547/t1548 both rc-marked live; the GM: meta filter doubly closes the door) + the bounded-shelf constraint from Fable's comments — [SOL](DOC/Research/Drift_risks_SOL.html) §R5. |

| 142 | **The #131 clock reconciler amplifies a wrong time-of-day word into a day skip — no cap on the forward roll.** TLDR: field case t1524 (2026-08-07, screenshot + t1525 export): scene was lunch on Day 6 at ~12:55 PM; the GM emitted `[TIME:mid-morning]` + `[TIME_ADVANCE:45]` — a one-word label error — and `clockReconcilePhase` (forward-only by design, clock.js:262) rolled to the NEXT mid-morning: **+1160m, Day 6 1:40 PM → Day 7 9:00 AM**, silently eating ~19h of every live countdown (the Collector deadline). The reconciler has NO cap: any backward-looking label costs up to ~24h. Candidate teeth (design fork): ① cap the top-up — a reconcile > ~6h is far likelier a mislabel than a timeskip → warn + SKIP (keep the [TIME:] text write, drop the clock roll), demanding a [REST:long] or explicit [TIME_ADVANCE:] for genuine overnight skips; ② treat a small-backward label (declared phase < ~6h behind the clock) as GM error → no-op + warn, reserving the forward roll for labels AHEAD of the clock. ⚠ Drift surface (#131 clock arithmetic) — Fable, test-first with the verbatim t1524 fixture. **Live-save repair available meanwhile** (desktop console, syncs to phone): `worldState.clock.min-=1160; worldState.world.time="early afternoon"; saveAll();` | S | Fable | ✅ **v1.563**, 2026-08-07 (greenlit after a 3-direction adversarial review — the user's "day of fishing" probe killed the flat 6h cap and produced the final shape). **Skip-and-DEMAND on the DAWN boundary** (days run dawn-to-dawn, so crossing dawn = entering tomorrow): a top-up whose declared phase already passed this engine-day (tgt<off) AND >RECONCILE_SKIP_MIN=6h keeps the [TIME:] text, rolls nothing, warns, pushes a visible muts line, and arms buildReconcileSkipNudge (one-shot, 2-turn shelf) demanding [REST:long] / [TIME_ADVANCE:Nd] / a corrected [TIME:] — bounding the forgotten-rest morning-after (the review's strongest counterexample) to ONE turn instead of a stuck clock. Honest same-day skips (fishing→dusk +10h) and small dawn-approaches (1am→dawn) reconcile as before. Base-rate evidence from the live ring: 1 reconcile in 40 responses and it was the misfire. The #131 founding dawn case is consciously SUPERSEDED (its test now pins skip→demand→REST:long healing, recorded in-test). Tests: verbatim t1524 + fishing/night-owl/morning-after battery, red-first → green. |

| 135 | **"Stay here while we go" splits die at birth — the #133b co-location purge is age-blind (Daeris field report, 2026-08-06).** TLDR: leaving a companion behind is the most common split, and the natural GM order ("Daeris stays at the inn" this response, the party departs next) writes a split record pointing at the party's current node — which the #133b auto-rejoin deletes in the SAME response, so by the time the party leaves there is no record: the panel shows her vitals (the user's sighting — Daeris HP/MP while she stayed at the inn) and the prompt lists her as present, contradicting the GM's own narration. **Mechanism** (scratch-reproduced, 3 scenarios): the co-location sweep (tag_table.js ~791, runs after every response's tags) folds ANY split whose loc+subloc exactly equals the party's node, with no way to distinguish a record written THIS response (a stay-behind ahead of departure — legitimate) from a pre-existing stale record (the church class it was built for). Split+departure in ONE response survives (the sweep runs after [LOCATION:] lands — the designed-for shape); split one turn early is purged loudly-but-invisibly (console.warn + one ephemeral muts line). World-only splits ([PARTY_SPLIT:Name\|Magnimar] sans subloc) survive to the immediate audit instead. ⚠ Drift surface (applyMuts write path + party/geography canon injection), SILENT to the player, a failure CLASS not a one-off. **Unproven for the live Daeris instance** (t1431 save predates the split; raw tags not persisted) — the GM-never-tagged-her alternative produces the identical end state; the repro proves the engine class either way. **Remedy sketch:** age-gate the purge — skip records with `splitLoc.turn===R.turn` (a same-response split-to-here is presumed a stay-behind; if the party genuinely does not leave, the NEXT response's sweep folds them back — deterministic, one-turn grace, no GM judgment). Red-first tests: Scenario A survives the write response AND stays split after the party's next-turn move; church-class stale record still folds on a later response; split+move-same-response and world-only-audit shapes unchanged. **Adjacent unguarded inverse, recorded not proposed:** a narrated stay-behind the GM never tags at all has NO teeth (phantom presence — the mirror of #133's stale-record class). | S | Fable | ✅ **v1.550**, 2026-08-06 (user greenlight "hit 135"). Fix is per-RESPONSE grace, deliberately NOT the row's `splitLoc.turn===R.turn` sketch: worldState.turn does not advance between applyMuts calls (tests, syncCharSheet), so a turn-stamp gate would have broken the church-arrival fold while sparing genuinely stale same-turn records. Instead the `[PARTY_SPLIT:]` handler marks names written THIS response on the R scratch (`R._freshSplits`) and the #133b sweep skips exactly those — a stay-behind survives its birth response and the departure; if the party genuinely goes nowhere, the NEXT response's sweep folds them back (deterministic one-response grace, no GM judgment). Test-first: 2 red → green (1050 total; the dies-at-birth repro + the grace-is-one-response bound), all three prior #133b shapes (church arrival / subloc exactness / world-only audit waiver) untouched-green. The adjacent never-tagged phantom-presence inverse remains recorded, not built. |

| 134 | **Missing-interior-description nudge — lived-in rooms get their canon pinned.** TLDR: the SECONDARY hardening from the t1431 multiplying-beds finding (sibling of #133, which carries the primary stale-splitLoc mechanism; full forensics in [Fable analysis](DOC/Research/t1431_drift_analysis_Fable.html) + [Sol analysis](DOC/Research/t1431_drift_analysis_Sol.html)): interior detail has exactly ONE pin, the write-once `[LOCATION_DESC:]`, and the GM filed it for 4/50 (8%) of the live save's sub-locations — an undescribed room's furniture lives only in prose, evaporates within ~2 summarize cycles, and gets re-imagined from genre priors on return. Latch stamps on fire (`worldState.locDescNudged`); re-fires every `LOC_DESC_NUDGE_COOLDOWN`=10 turns while still null (#29 rot lesson); a filed description ends it permanently; silent in combat; fires only where the party actually dwells. ⚠ Drift surface (engine-note channel) — Fable, test-first. Ship-time confidence, recorded honestly: HIGH that descriptions get filed (the #20/#129 channel is field-proven), MODERATE that furniture drift fully stops (depends on the GM recording countable facts; and per #133 the t1431 incident had a stronger co-cause this nudge does not touch). | S | Fable | ✅ **v1.541**, 2026-08-04. Test-first: 3 engine tests failing → green (1023 total). Field verification: next lived-in undescribed room produces a description within ~2 turns of settling. |

| 132 | **Output-truncation detection — a response cut mid-tag must be loud, not a silent mutation loss plus raw text on screen.** TLDR: the engine has ZERO truncation detection — no provider adapter reads `stop_reason`/`finish_reason` — so when the 1000-token output cap cuts a GM response mid-tag, the trailing fragment renders RAW to the player (`cleanTxt`'s strip regexes require the closing `]`), the mutation is silently lost (all TAG_TABLE handlers match complete tags), and no warn fires anywhere (`__tagUnknownScan` needs a colon). Field case: Runelords t1410 (B21 evidence) ended `…was listening. [SCH` — the GM's demanded `[SCHEDULE_RESOLVED:…]` was cut and lost; harmless there only because the sweep retired the entry anyway, but a truncated `[QUEST:x\|completed]` would silently drop the completion AND leak raw text. Direction (B21 findings sketch ③): each provider's `parseResponse` also surfaces its finish reason; on length-truncation `callGM`/the commit path warns loudly (console + toast + #16 crumb) and strips the trailing unterminated `\[[A-Z_]*:?[^\]]*$` fragment from the DISPLAYED text only (transcript keeps the raw truth). ⚠ Drift surface (cleanTxt + frozen strip hashes, provider adapters, response-commit path) — Fable tier, design review before code; a greedy trailing-fragment strip could eat legitimate bracketed prose, so the regex must anchor to end-of-response. | S | Fable | ✅ **v1.538**, 2026-08-03. `parseFinish` on all 5 provider adapters (OpenAI-shaped share `OPENAI_FINISH`); callGM warns loud (console + toast + #16 crumb) on a length-cap finish; cleanTxt drops a trailing unterminated ≥3-caps tag fragment (end-anchored; complete tags and lowercase bracket prose untouched; sessionLog keeps the raw). Test-first: 2 tests failing → green (1016). |
| 131 | **Time desync — the free-text time-of-day and the campaign clock disagree in front of the GM.** TLDR: the game currently keeps TWO independent time surfaces and they've drifted apart in the field — in the B21 screenshot (Runelords t1410) the membar clock reads "Day 5, 3:15 PM" (`clockStamp()`, the #73 monotonic clock) while `worldState.world.time` — the free-text field written only by the `[TIME:]` tag and the Sync modal — still says "dawn", and the narrative itself reads as *evening* ("the day's already worn thin at the edges"). Both surfaces are injected into the GM prompt every turn (world-state block carries `world.time`; `buildClockBlock` carries the stamp), so the GM is served contradictory time context — a canon-drift feeder, not just a display blemish. The #106b one-formatter rule ("session bar and story frames can never disagree") covered the clock's own surfaces but never brought `world.time` into the fold. Design fork to settle before code: subordinate/derive the free-text label from `clockTimeOfDay()` (one writer, `[TIME:]` becomes advisory), or make `[TIME:]` writes reconcile the clock (a "dawn" write advances to the next dawn boundary — the #89 dawn-anchor rule already defines it), or retire `world.time` injection entirely. ⚠ Drift surface (tag vocabulary + prompt canon-injection + clock) — Fable tier, design review before code. | M | Fable | ✅ **v1.539**, 2026-08-03 (user go-ahead "hit the clock conflict issues"; fork resolved: **reconcile, not derive/retire** — `[TIME:]` stays the GM's narrative channel and the ENGINE walks the clock forward to the declared phase). `TIME_PHASES` + `clockReconcilePhase` (clock.js): 10 phase keywords with target minute + an already-true band (dawn=0≡6am, #89 anchor), matched most-specific-first; called from the applyMutsTable TAIL so same-response `[TIME_ADVANCE:]`/`[REST:long]` land first — a consistent pair no-ops via the band, only a genuine desync tops up, forward-only (monotonic — a backward declaration rolls to the NEXT occurrence). Unmappable free text stays flavor (stored, clock untouched). Loud muts line on every reconcile. NOT taken (recorded): deriving/retiring `world.time` injection (GM keeps its narrative channel); editing the `[TIME:]` STATE-TAGS doc line (hash-pinned, behavior is engine-side); Sync-modal manual time edits still don't touch the clock (deliberate override). The field save's standing "dawn"-vs-3:15pm desync heals on the GM's next mappable `[TIME:]` write. Test-first: 4 engine tests incl. the verbatim field numbers (clock 6315 + `[TIME:dawn]` → 7200) → green, 1020 total. |

No data migration, no pruning — label only. Verified against the real save: the boundary lands at exactly index 111 on Ammut's 130-beat array (the Sandpoint-burns beat is the first native one). | 129 | **Event & clock hygiene — schedule teeth (escalate then expire) + zero-objective quest nudge.** TLDR: a scheduled deadline the GM never resolves used to haunt the prompt forever — the live save served "Tide turns against the return route" as HAPPENING NOW for ~1,100 turns (due at clock minute 357, still injected at minute 6,005), because removal depended on the GM emitting `[SCHEDULE_RESOLVED:]` unprompted — the same GM-never-volunteers-resolution channel failure #29 fixed for futureEvents, in its second instance (house rule: fix the class). Also ships the review's quest gap: an ACTIVE quest with zero objectives gave the player no checklist and the #20 teeth nothing to detect — `stampQuestCompletion` now stamps `noObjSince` and `buildQuestObjectiveNudge` demands a first `[QUEST_STEP:]` after `QUEST_OBJECTIVE_NUDGE_TURNS` (3). Both notes: one per turn (stalest), silent in combat, offered quests exempt (not accepted goals). The live save's stale entry heals on its first turn after deploy — the exact field numbers (due 357, clock 6005) are an engine test. Prompt + cache split untouched (notes ride the user message; stable-half purity suite green). | M | Fable | ✅ **v1.526**, 2026-08-02. Test-first: 8 engine tests written failing (incl. the verbatim field case), then implementation to green; 976 total. **⚠ B21 follow-up v1.537 (2026-08-03): the go-live "heal turn" itself misfired in the field** — an entry past BOTH thresholds earned one narrate-the-consequence command before the sweep, and the GM confabulated a present companion trapped at the stale event (Frizwick/tide, Runelords t1410). Fixed expire-before-escalate: `buildScheduleEscalation` and the clock block's HAPPENING NOW now skip past-expiry entries (full record: DOC/BUGS.md B21). |

| 113 | **Whisper STT exploration — where should speech-to-text transcription actually run?** TLDR: today's STT is two paths (stt.js): native Web Speech API where it exists (Chrome/Edge desktop + Android; NOT Firefox, NOT iOS Safari), and a cloud fallback that records the utterance and sends it to OpenAI `whisper-1` **on the player's own OpenAI key** — the path that makes iPhone Safari / Car Mode work at all, and the key-ownership property that was decisive in declining LiveKit. Exploration (findings doc first, NO code): ① **Local WASM Whisper** (whisper.cpp / transformers.js) — the Piper #41 pattern: vendored, offline, $0, model cached in OPFS; questions: tiny/base model size (~40-150MB) vs fantasy-noun accuracy, cold-start + per-utterance latency on a phone, and WASM memory pressure on iOS Safari (the r8/jetsam monotonic-resources lesson — soak test, not one input). ② **Self-hosted Whisper on the tnd-tts Fly app** — the #90 M1 pattern: same warm-daemon/volume machinery, auth via the game session token, removes the OpenAI-key requirement for connected players; questions: per-utterance round-trip vs the 15s MediaRecorder cap, cost on our box, and whether the STT ladder should mirror `TTS_LADDER` (native → server → cloud-on-player-key → none). ③ **Status quo** as the control. Decision criteria: the iOS Safari / Car Mode path is the critical consumer (#78 Phase 2 wants spoken 1/2/3 action picking), accuracy on fantasy proper nouns (measure with and without the `sttCorrectNames` backstop — relates #77), and who pays. Output: a DOC/ findings page + a go/no-go per option; any build work becomes its own row. | M | Opus | ✅ **Exploration complete 2026-08-03** — findings: [DOC/Research/DOC_whisper_stt.html](DOC/Research/DOC_whisper_stt.html). Verdicts: local WASM **NO-GO** (least accuracy, worst latency, second iOS wasm memory ratchet — the B9 class); self-hosted on Fly **DEFER** to the subscription proxy era; status quo + four upgrades **GO** (send Whisper's prompt-bias param with roster/location names — we never have; model swap whisper-1 → gpt-4o-mini-transcribe with fallback; WebAudio silence endpointing replacing the 15s wall; mic-path constraints audit + narrowband-BT telemetry into #16 crumbs). Key car finding: the dominant factor is likely the Bluetooth hands-free mic (telephone-band capture) — engine-independent, measure-then-mitigate. **§4 upgrades SHIPPED v1.536 (user go 2026-08-03):** prompt-bias on every request (sttBiasPrompt, helpers — engine-tested; party/roster/places/quests, deduped, 800-char cap at a name boundary), gpt-4o-mini-transcribe primary with a LOUD whisper-1 retry + #16 crumb, WebAudio silence endpointing (1.5s quiet after speech ends the take; 45s hard cap replaces the 15s wall; noisy-cabin floor degrades to cap-only, never a cut-off; singleton ctx per the monotonic-resources rule), and mic getSettings telemetry (stt-mic crumb + one-per-session narrowband toast in Car Mode). 5-clause STT UPGRADES CONTRACT sabotage-proven 5/5. Honest limit: the mic itself can't be harness-driven — the next real car session is the field test; watch the stt-mic crumbs' sampleRate. Local-WASM/self-host verdicts stand as before. |

| 92 | **Compress the transcript inside the cloud-sync payload — the 2 MB toast fired in the field.** TLDR: the sync POST ships `worldState` with the transcript as a PLAIN array, so a mature campaign uploads its whole history every synced turn — the sentinel toast ("⚠ Cloud sync upload is 2.0 MB — mature campaign; mention it to the dev", storage-adapter.js `_syncSizeWarned`) fired for real on the Runelords save at t1130 (2026-07-24). No data risk (server body cap is 8 MB, so 25% used), but the payload grows forever and the per-turn upload cost on cellular is the real bill. **Fix = "optimize the copy, never the source" (sanctioned by the transcript decree):** send the `serializeWorldState()` compressed form (`transcript` → `{__lz:…}`) in `syncToServer` — the server stores TEXT + `json_extract('$.turn')` which the compressed form doesn't touch. ⚠ **CORRECTED by the 2026-07-24 evidence pass (brief E): the server-ADOPT path consumes the pulled blob RAW (`worldState = data.worldState`, storage-adapter) — it never goes through `parseWorldState`, so the "read side is already tolerant" claim is FALSE for exactly the path that matters.** The pull hops (reconcile adopt + campaign-picker fetch) must inflate the compressed form too, or shipping this poisons live state. Also bundle: the transcript-memo enforcement decision (entry-4 ★ verdict — generalized stamp accessor vs. revision counter; the `rc`-adjacency and hardcoded-array fragilities are recorded there). Verify the WHOLE round trip: pull path (`load`/`fillPortraitsFromBlob`), campaign-list derivation (server reads `world_state` for char/level/location — those fields stay plain), `.tnd` export/import unaffected, CAS guard, and a device on an OLD version pulling a compressed blob (parseWorldState tolerance predates this, but PROVE it with the real failure case). Expected: ~2.0 MB → well under 1 MB; the 8 MB horizon moves out by thousands of turns. ⚠ Save-data transport — a compressor bug here IS data loss; engine-test the round trip against a real mature blob. | S-M | Fable | ✅ **SHIPPED v1.504** (2026-07-30, Fable). **The wire format IS the disk format:** `compressWorldStateSnapshot()` (state.js) is the ONE producer — extracted from `serializeWorldState` (now a pure veneer, byte-identical output, SHARED memo so a saveCore-then-sync turn pays one LZ pass) — and both POST paths (`_syncNow` payload + `pushCampaignState`) route through it. **The adopt-hop poison the row flagged is closed:** `inflateWorldStateSnapshot()` (the object-form twin of `parseWorldState`, which is now a veneer over it) runs FIRST in the reconcile before any consumer touches `data.worldState`; the campaign-pull hops needed no change (their slot writes pass {__lz} through and `parseWorldState` inflates on read — verified by test). **Measured on the real 2,527-entry Ammut blob: worldState wire 1.93 MB → 1.09 MB (transcript alone 1.72 → 0.87 MB), full deep-equal round trip.** The UTF-16 form costs ~3 bytes/char in UTF-8 — a base64 wire would shave ~17% more but introduces a SECOND format old clients cannot read, so one-format-everywhere won (mixed-version safety beats the extra bytes). **Old-client proof (the row demanded it):** a pre-#92 device adopting the compressed blob raw suffers only session-transient breakage — its own `serializeWorldState` passthrough stores {__lz} intact and its `parseWorldState` (v1.227+) inflates on next load, ZERO permanent loss — engine-tested as the OLD-CLIENT SAFETY test. 6 tests + a 3-clause SYNC COMPRESSION CONTRACT in run-tests.js (the async reconcile is source-pinned); 6/6 sabotage clauses proven on a scratch copy. The transcript-memo enforcement bundle (entry-4 ★) deliberately NOT folded in — no new fragility was introduced (the helper shares the existing memo), so that decision stays its own item. |

| 94 | **Test Piper's "high" voices — the quality upgrade we may already own.** TLDR: Kokoro (#91) died on cost, but the engine already shipping has a better-sounding tier we've never evaluated. `PIPER_VOICES` already lists four **high**-quality models — `en_US-libritts-high` (904 speakers, **already downloaded on the server volume**), `en_US-lessac-high`, `en_US-ryan-high`, `en_GB-cori-high` — against the **medium** models in use today. Same engine, same warm-daemon machinery, same endpoint, same client: switching is a *voice pick*, not an architecture change. **The only open question is CPU cost**, and the headroom is enormous: Piper medium measures RTF 0.16 on the live box, so a high model could be **6× slower and still clear the ≥1× realtime bar** (where Kokoro needed to get 2× *faster* and failed at 2.1). **The work:** point `tts/bench-kokoro.mjs`'s corpus at the piper daemon instead (or a small sibling script — the 30-unit corpus and the RTF/percentile reporting are reusable as-is), measure medium vs high on identical text, then **listen** — the ear check is the actual deliverable, since the whole point is "does it sound meaningfully better." ⚠ Run it when the box is otherwise idle: the #91 run proved sustained CPU exhausts the `shared-cpu-1x` burst balance, and a benchmark competing with live narration corrupts both. If high lands under ~0.6 RTF and sounds better, it ships as a narrator/character voice preference with **zero code change** (and #91 closes as "quality upgrade delivered by a different route"); if it's slower than that, the per-character casting question becomes "which voices are worth the CPU." ✅ **FIELD DATA ALREADY ANSWERS THE SPEED HALF (2026-07-24, no benchmark needed):** the live session was found to be *already synthesizing with* `en_US-libritts-high` (a warm daemon at rate 1.05), so the server's own logs measure it directly — **high ≈ RTF 0.98–1.04 vs medium ≈ 0.16–0.21**, i.e. **~6× the CPU cost, landing at break-even** (1s of CPU per 1s of speech). It WORKS on the shipped box — but with essentially zero margin, where medium has 6×. ⚠ Read those log lines correctly: piper reports **cumulative** infer/audio per daemon, so each line is a running average for that voice's whole session, not one sentence (the monotonically rising "20s audio" figures are totals — no single unit is anywhere near the client's 10s per-unit timeout). **What remains is therefore a JUDGMENT call, not a measurement:** ① does high sound meaningfully better to the user's ear (the actual deliverable); ② is break-even margin acceptable as a *default* — the exposure is concurrent narration (multiplayer/second device) or a long unbroken read pushing past 1.0, where the client's lookahead drains and units start missing the timeout; ③ per-character casting economics — at 6× cost, which characters are worth a high voice. Safest shape if the ear says yes: **high for the narrator (or for one or two signature characters), medium elsewhere** — the per-character voice system already supports exactly that mix, with no code change. ⚠ Also note the #91 burst lesson applies at the margin: sustained RTF 1.0 pegs the core for the whole read, which is the load pattern that exhausts the shared-CPU balance; between-turn idle is what refills it. | S | Any | Filed 2026-07-24 from the #91 benchmark's most useful side finding; **speed half answered same day by field logs** — remaining work is an ear check + a default-voice decision ✅ **Closed 2026-07-30 — evaluated and confirmed good (user: checked and rechecked).** The ear check happened and the outcome stands; the shipped voice set and default are keepers. → **Assigned to Sol (Codex) 2026-08-03** — brief in [DOC/todos_completed/SOL_TASKS.md](DOC/todos_completed/SOL_TASKS.md). |

| 11 | **Prompt caching (Anthropic `cache_control`)** — cache the stable prefix of the system prompt so it re-reads at ~0.1× input cost instead of full price every turn. **Do as its own focused task** (touches load-bearing prompt construction). **Why it's not a one-liner:** `buildSysPrompt` is rebuilt and *changes every turn* (turn counter, HP, gold, location, memory TOC, geography, quests), and currently **interleaves** stable content (rules, STATE TAGS block, STYLE, prose voice) with volatile content (char sheet, world, memory) — so a naive whole-prompt cache gets ~0 hits and pays the 1.25× write penalty for nothing. **Plan:** (1) reorder `buildSysPrompt` so ALL static content comes first (role decl, `getRulesBlock()`, tone, full STATE TAGS + companion-tag instructions, STYLE, prose voice — ~2–4k tokens), (2) drop one `cache_control:{type:"ephemeral"}` breakpoint at the end of that static block, (3) put ALL per-turn volatile state after it (char sheet, world, NPCs, memory, geography, quests, combat), (4) change `PROVIDERS.anthropic.buildBody` to send `system` as a two-block array (stable+cache_control, then volatile) instead of one string. **Thresholds:** min cacheable prefix 2048 tok (Sonnet 4.6) / **4096 (Opus 4.8)** — the static block must clear it or it silently won't cache. **Verify with a node render-diff** that the stable prefix is byte-identical turn-to-turn (any stray timestamp/turn-counter leak into the prefix kills caching) and check `usage.cache_read_input_tokens`>0. TTL 5min (warm during active play; one cold write after idle). **Anthropic-only** — lives in `PROVIDERS.anthropic.buildBody`; OpenAI caches automatically, Grok/Gemini differ. Directly serves #24 + the subscription cost model. | M | — | ✅ Done (v1.151) — `buildSysPrompt` returns `{stable, volatile}` (stable: rules → adult → role → tone+note → narrative DNA → MECHANICS/STATE TAGS/companion tags; volatile: identity → sheet → world/party → quests/skeleton → memory → geo → combat/history → REMINDER → **STYLE, kept last for voice fidelity**); Anthropic `buildBody` sends a two-block `system` array with `cache_control:ephemeral` on the stable block; other providers flatten via `sysJoin()`; `sysOverride` strings unchanged. 6 new engine tests incl. the byte-identical-stable invariant (70 total). **Measured (12-turn harness runs on the Runelords t54 save, Sonnet 4.6, telemetry #21):** stable block = 4,889 tok; total prompt/turn identical before vs after (12,160 vs 12,162 tok — clean A/B). Cold write turn 1, then **11/11 cache hits** (reads = exactly 11×4,889). Turn-bucket cost **$0.5056 → $0.3594 (−28.9%)**; input-only cost/turn −32.3%; all-in batch (turns+actions+summarize) $0.599 → $0.450 (−24.9%). Caveat: 5-min TTL — a player idling >5 min between turns pays a re-write (1.25×) instead of a read; worst case ≈ +0.4¢/turn over uncached, negligible. Prose voice spot-checked, held. Future headroom: 2nd breakpoint on slow-moving volatile chunks (skeleton/memory TOC) or message-history caching. |

| 15 | **Blueprint cloud library** — store campaign blueprints (full `.campaign` packages) in the cloud so they're available across your devices (a **PERSONAL** library — community *sharing* was scrapped 2026-07-08; see Architecture ▸ "Community blueprint sharing — scrapped"). A blueprint is the complete campaign authoring package: skeleton (3-act narrative spine + arcs), seeded NPCs with roles/knowledge, locations with canonical descriptions, custom rules, starting region, and blueprint name. It is NOT a save file — no player character, no combat state, no XP/gold. **Two save surfaces:** (1) upload a local `.campaign` file to the library ("☁ Save to library" alongside the file import on the Tone step); (2) **Export from active game** — File menu item that packages the current `worldState.skeleton`, `worldState.npcs[]` (minus the player PC), `memory.locations`, `customRules`, `worldState.world.region`, and `worldState.campName` into a portable blueprint (for your own reuse across devices, or to feed the curated-catalog pipeline #44). **Two load surfaces:** (1) cloud tab in a blueprint browser modal on the Tone step; (2) local `.campaign` file tab (existing flow). **Server:** new `blueprints` table mirroring the `characters` table pattern — `(user_id, slug, name, blueprint_data, updated_at)`. Three endpoints: `GET /api/blueprints`, `POST /api/blueprints`, `DELETE /api/blueprints/:slug`. **Storage adapter:** `listBlueprintLibrary(cb)`, `saveBlueprintToLibrary(bp, cb)`, `deleteBlueprintFromLibrary(slug, cb)`. **Export sanitization:** strip act/arc `status` back to `pending`/`active` on the first act (so it plays fresh); exclude `worldState.character` and all per-run state; exclude `worldState.transcript`. | L | Any | ⚠ Mostly done — **audit 2026-07-04 found far more built than this row admitted:** server `blueprints` table (WITH the `public` column) + all 3 endpoints live on Fly (verified 401 from deployed `/api/blueprints`); `storageAdapter.listBlueprintLibrary/saveBlueprintToLibrary/deleteBlueprintFromLibrary` shipped; game-side save + browse surfaces exist (ui.js library modal + save-to-library). **v1.174 added the Blueprint Designer surfaces: ☁ Publish (validate → upsert to personal library, same name republishes) + ☁ Library… (list with PUBLIC badge/date/act count, Open into editor, delete w/ confirm).** Auth rides in from the app's login via same-origin localStorage (`autoConnect`); disconnected state points at File ▸ Connect. Wire verified against the LIVE server (bogus-token 401 path end-to-end in preview). **Community browse: SCRAPPED (2026-07-08)** — see Architecture ▸ "Community blueprint sharing — scrapped". The server's `public` column stays dormant (no browse-public endpoint, never flips for user content). Personal library is feature-complete; the sanctioned content path is now **curated official blueprints (#44)**. <br><br>**ROW CLOSED 2026-07-27 (audit):** everything scoped here shipped long since (server table + endpoints + adapter + both save/load surfaces + Designer publish/library); the only live blueprint thread is #44's alpha starter set. |

| 24 | **Sync hardening (client) — timeout, retry, visible failure state.** Born from the 2026-07-03 incident: a Fly host died, every sync POST from the phone hung, and the FIRST hang permanently wedged `_syncing=true` in storage-adapter.js — all later syncs (incl. the beforeunload/visibilitychange flush) early-returned as `_pendingSync`, so 20 turns accumulated locally with the UI showing "Connected" the whole time. Work: (a) **timeout the state POST** (~20s, AbortController) so a hung fetch always resolves and resets `_syncing`; (b) **retry** on next saveAll / `online` event / app-foreground — local is source of truth, so one successful POST after recovery uploads the complete current state, no replay needed; (c) **visible failure state** — track `lastAckedTurn` (set only on 2xx), and when `worldState.turn` pulls ahead show a red ☁ + "N turns unsynced" in the membar, toast after consecutive failures, and a dedicated "session expired — reconnect" toast on 401 (currently every failure is console.warn, invisible on mobile); (d) apply the same timeout to `syncPortrait` and the campaign-list GET (the latter's 60s timeout is why "Waking server up" flashes forever against a dead host). | S | — | ✅ Done (v1.152) — `_tFetch` (AbortController, 20s) wraps the state POST, portrait PUT, and campaign-list GET (old 60s picker timer replaced). `_syncOk`/`_onSyncFail` track `_lastAckTurn` + consecutive `_failCount`; `syncStatus()` exposed; ui.js `updateSyncBadge()` renders a red ☁ membar badge ("N turns unsynced" / "sync failing"), refreshed every turn AND on every sync result. Toasts: once at 3 consecutive failures, dedicated "Session expired — reconnect" on 401 (once per outage). Retry on `online` event + app-foreground (when unsynced>0); `load()` seeds `_lastAckTurn` from the server's turn so a device that's AHEAD shows the gap immediately. storage-adapter.js now loads in the headless test suite (72 assertions). **Fault-injection verified live in preview:** connection-refused ×3 → badge + single toast; bogus token vs real server → 401 toast; hanging fetch (dead-host clone) → aborted at 20s, `_syncing` reset, next sync attempted. The 2026-07-03 failure mode now self-heals in 20 seconds and is visible from turn one. |

| 27 | **RAG-based episodic memory** — retrieval over the verbatim transcript to fight summary-loss drift (details vanish in chapter compression) and saturation drift (rule collapse under an ever-growing `memoryTOC`, the 0/54 naming failure). Design discussion held 2026-07-03; full decisions in [RAG_MEMORY.md](DOC/RAG_MEMORY.md). Headlines: **augment don't replace** (structured memory stays authoritative for current truth; retrieval carries episodic texture ONLY — else you trade for retrieval-miss + stale-chunk drift); **entity-keyed retrieval, not vectors** (transcript entries tagged at write time from the tags `applyMuts` already parses; retrieval = scene ∪ input entities; embeddings are a maybe-never Phase 2); **read-side only** (summarize() untouched — the one irreversible failure is weakening write-side); **memoryTOC diet ships behind the same flag** (the real anti-drift win is prompt decongestion, and it pays for the excerpt tokens); **Dev Mode kill switch per campaign** (flag off must reproduce today's TOC byte-for-byte — engine test). Payoff is back-loaded: judge on a mature save, never a fresh harness run. | M | — | ✅ Phase 1 done (v1.154) — entity index written on every GM transcript entry (`logTranscript` 3rd param, raw tags + known-NPC name scan; lazy backfill for old entries), `ragRetrieve()` (memory.js) scores by scene∪input entity overlap, skips the last 10 turns, ≤3 excerpts / ~600 tok, oldest-first, turn-stamped, "current state overrides" framing; injected in `buildSysPrompt`'s VOLATILE half only; `memoryTOC` diet behind the same flag (lore → scene-relevant + recent 8 cap 12; chapter summaries dropped as duplicates of STORY SO FAR). | 28 | **Summarize-tail retention — kill the amnesia cliff.** Finding from the t160 Runelords analysis (2026-07-04): with prose-voice responses at 1,300–3,100 chars, `SUMMARIZE_AT` (1200 tok, tuned in the 2-3-sentence-cap era) fires **every ~2 turns** in mature campaigns (chapter stamps 152/155/157/159), so the GM's verbatim window is ~2 turns deep and `summarize()` clears `sessionLog` to ZERO — object-level facts (the Mage-Hand pin grab, t146) evaporate within a few turns, and the GM *confabulates* when asked to recall them (t160's "correction" invented a false version of the grab — wrong place, wrong scene). **Fix: after a successful summarize, retain the most recent 2–3 exchanges in `sessionLog` instead of clearing it** — extraction unchanged (no RAG write-side violation), the cliff becomes a slope, scene continuity survives the boundary. Also halves the summarize cadence (retained tail re-counts toward the threshold — verify the loop can't thrash). Engine-testable. Side benefit: summarize firing every 2 turns is also a 2,000-token call every 2 turns — cadence relief is cost relief. | S | — | ✅ Done (v1.165) — `retainSessionTail()` (memory.js) keeps the newest ≤3 exchanges within a 1,600-token cap (newest pair always survives) instead of clearing; `worldState.sessKept` marks the already-extracted head so `sessionTokens()` counts only NEW tokens (retained tail can't re-trip the gate — no thrash) and the extraction prompt slices from the marker (nothing filed to memory twice — a stronger guarantee than the spec's "tail re-counts" sketch, which would have double-extracted). Cadence relief delivered via `SUMMARIZE_AT` 1200→2400 (safe now the boundary has no cliff): fires every ~5 mature exchanges instead of ~2. Degraded-failure path retains too. Stale markers fail safe (`sessKeptStart()`). 6 new engine tests (97 total) + full two-cycle boundary simulation against the t198 save: 3 exchanges retained, zero re-extraction, zero lost exchanges, RAG skipN follows the deeper window. |

| 29 | **futureEvents hygiene — stale "pending" pollution.** t160 save: `memory.futureEvents` pegged at the 30 cap, with long-resolved items still pending ("combat with six chanters," "investigate the altar," "far passage" — all completed turns earlier); the GM rarely emits `[FUTURE_EVENT_RESOLVED:]` on its own, so PENDING EVENTS injects finished business every turn — attention noise in an already-saturated prompt. Options (combine): ① prompt nudge — the STATE TAGS block tells the GM to resolve events it narrates as done; ② auto-expire — drop unresolved events older than ~40 turns (`setTurn` is already stored); ③ have `summarize()` also return a `resolvedEvents[]` list (it reads the whole session anyway). Start with ②, it's free and deterministic. **t198 escalation (2026-07-04): 7 of the 30 pending slots are "find Shalelu" variants (set t159–t189) while Shalelu joined the party at ~t189 — near-duplicate spam on file is half the problem; add dedupe in `fileFutureEvent` (token-overlap against existing pending) to the work list.** | S | — | ✅ Done (v1.166) — ②+③+dedupe shipped (① prompt nudge skipped: ③ covers it without spending stable-prompt attention). `fileFutureEvent` near-dup dedupe: ≥2 shared stemmed tokens (`feTokens`) AND ≥half the smaller fingerprint → refresh the existing entry's `setTurn` instead of filing a twin. `expireFutureEvents()` drops unresolved events older than `FUTURE_EXPIRE_TURNS`=40 (globals.js) at every summarize; unstamped entries grandfathered. Extraction prompt now lists pending events under "ANTICIPATED EVENTS"; the extractor echoes finished ones as EXACT text into `resolvedEvents[]` → `resolveFutureEvent` removes them. Filing refactored into sync `applySummaryExtract()` (testable without an API call); order expire→file→resolve so a same-window set-and-finished event nets out removed. Verified against the t198 save: 7 Shalelu variants → 2 genuinely-distinct phrasings, both Hemlock items survive, backlog decays 26→6 by t230, and a stubbed end-to-end summarize resolves an echoed item. 6 new engine tests (103 total). |

| 84 | **Explore LiveKit.** TLDR: look at whether LiveKit is worth adopting for real-time audio, and if so for what.
