Obvious candidate uses to weigh: networked co-op (#1 Q7, the one open multiplayer question — LiveKit is a WebRTC room/SFU stack, so it would cover presence + transport, not the turn arbitration hot-seat already solves); voice INPUT beyond the Web Speech API (#77 lives here — STT is Chrome/Edge only today); and streamed cloud TTS, which is worth thinking about carefully given the whole #9 rework just landed on ON-DEVICE Piper for $0 and offline play, so anything cloud-shaped reopens a cost/ownership decision the user has already made once. Investigation should establish: what it actually buys over what ships today, what it costs (it is a hosted service — server-side, so it lands on the Fly.dev side and in the subscription model), whether it needs an account/keys per player, and whether it works on the iPhone Safari + Car Mode path that is the primary use case. **Design-first; no code.** — **OUTCOME 2026-07-21: INVESTIGATED AND DECLINED (user call).** Full report: **[DOC/Research/liveKit_findings.html](DOC/Research/liveKit_findings.html)** (31-agent research pass — 25 researchers → 5 supervisors → 1 synthesizer). Three findings settled it. **(a) LiveKit does no STT itself** — transcription lives in the Agents framework, which needs an always-on server worker plus server-signed JWTs, so none of it is reachable from a static, no-build, no-npm, ES5.1 client; the whole benefit list is conditional on an architecture we don't have. **(b) Every gap it was raised to close is already closed in-repo** — the iPhone Safari path by the `MediaRecorder → whisper-1` fallback in stt.js (v1.342), fantasy proper nouns by `sttCorrectNames` (v1.330), and multiplayer by hot-seat on ONE device (#1 — there is no networking to attach voice to yet). The research lanes were briefed off a stale CLAUDE.md and spent most of their effort solving solved problems; **the CLAUDE.md `stt.js`/`tts.js` rows were corrected in the same commit as this row.** **(c) Cost inverts who pays** — today the player spends their own key; LiveKit bills the *developer* ~$0.60/player-hour in agent-session fees dominated by connection time rather than speech, reopening exactly the ownership decision #9 already settled on on-device Piper for $0. One genuine capability gap survives — coordinated turn-taking / barge-in for Car Mode — and LiveKit's own issue tracker has that closed *not planned*, so adopting it wouldn't fix it either. Re-open triggers listed in §8 of the findings doc (the honest one: if #1 ever grows real cross-device networking, the transport question is worth re-asking — but as transport, not as STT). | S (investigation) | Sonnet | 🚫 **Closed — investigated and DECLINED** (2026-07-21, user call) — see [DOC/Research/liveKit_findings.html](DOC/Research/liveKit_findings.html) |
| 86 | **Rework the TODO format — it has outgrown one-row-per-task, and the rows have become unreadable.** TLDR: #9 was a single 16,000-character table cell with no paragraph breaks; the user's verdict was that it made their eyes bleed, and they were right. Raised 2026-07-22. **Two separate problems, worth naming apart.** <br><br>**① NO SUBTASKS.** A big feature accretes ratified decisions, build-log entries, findings and deferred ideas into ONE cell, so there is nowhere to record "this part is done, that part is not" except more prose. #9 currently carries a ratified design (⑤ items), seven shipped-version entries, two deferred enhancements and now a field report — all as one blob, all sharing a single Status. Subtasks with their own status are the actual missing structure. <br><br>**② NO PARAGRAPHS.** Partly a tooling artefact: `dev/lint-todo.js` requires each row to be ONE physical line (a raw newline in a cell breaks GFM and strands every row below — the corruption it exists to catch), so writers default to one unbroken run. `<br>` is the sanctioned escape and the linter's own error message says so, but nothing enforced its use. **Applied as a stopgap 2026-07-22:** #9 broken into 14 paragraphs with `<br><br>`, content verified byte-identical. That helps the symptom; it does not give subtasks. <br><br>**Design questions for the rework:** does a task become its own section (heading + prose + a small status table) rather than a table row, which would remove the one-physical-line constraint entirely? If so, what reads the file — `todo-viewer.html` parses the tables, and `dev/lint-todo.js` guards them, so both move with the format. Is there a per-subtask status, or just checkboxes? Does the history (the shipped-version log that makes #9 huge) belong in the task at all, or should it move to a changelog and leave the task describing only what is LEFT? That last one is probably the biggest single win and the least work. <br><br>**Constraint worth respecting:** the current format's one virtue is that it is greppable and machine-checkable, and the linter has caught real corruption from at least two writers (viewer exports, hand/model edits). Do not trade that away for prettiness. | M | Sonnet | 🚫 **IGNORED (user ruling 2026-07-27)** — no format rework; the one-row-per-task table + `<br><br>` paragraphs + lint stays as is |
| 87 | **The Piper phonemizer's reuse patch fails open into the v1.323 leak, permanently and silently.** TLDR: `tndPhonemize` (vendor/piper/vits/vits-web.js) caches ONE Emscripten phonemizer module and re-drives it via `callMain`; if any call throws it sets `tndPhon.broken = true` and falls back to building a FRESH 16MB module per synth — which is exactly the v1.323 leak the cache exists to prevent. **Nothing ever resets the latch**, so one bad sentence downgrades the rest of the page's life, and the only complaint is a `console.warn` a phone cannot show. <br><br> **Found in a lab, not the field (2026-07-22, while making ORT memory observable for B9):** the new wasm probe counted **27 phonemizer modules over a 100-synth soak** where the design allows one, with `memory access out of bounds` in the console. ⚠ **The TRIGGER is unproven on real narration** — it fired on long synthetic word-salad sentences from the soak generator, so it may be an artifact of that input. The latch, the missing reset and the silent downgrade are plain in the code regardless of what trips them. <br><br> **The field answer is already instrumented:** v1.416 puts the module count on the narration-death crumb as `pn`, so the next real crumb says whether this happens in play. **Decide on that evidence before coding.** Remedies, cheapest first: (1) reset the latch after N synths or on the next read so one fault is not permanent; (2) make the downgrade LOUD (toast + `reportError`), since a silent fallback into a known leak class violates the no-silent-failures policy; (3) find and fix what makes `callMain` fault on re-entry. ⚠ Any `vendor/piper/*` edit MUST bump `PIPER_RUNTIME_REV` (r9 today) or the permanent SW cache eats the patch. | S–M | Fable | 🚫 **CLOSED 2026-07-27 (user ruling): parked pending field evidence — no `pn`>1 crumb in 5 days of watching, and #90 (v1.435, shipped 3 days into the watch) moved connected-play synthesis to the server, shrinking the local phonemizer's trigger surface to offline reads and ladder degrades.** The lab trigger was synthetic word-salad input, always suspect. Decide whether punctuation is APPENDED at render (`buildActionButtons`/`generateActions`, game.js — deterministic, also covers stored `worldState.lastActions` from old saves) or requested from the model in `SUGGESTION_MODE_BLOCK` (risks over-punctuating). ⚠ The suggestion pipeline carries the v1.288 user watch flag — keep the stable half byte-identical; touch only the render/parse side. | S | Sonnet | ✅ **Done (v1.454, 2026-07-27, Sonnet).** Went with the deterministic render-time option — the prompt (`SUGGESTION_MODE_BLOCK`/`buildSuggestionSys`) is untouched, so the v1.288 watch flag has nothing to trip. New pure helper `punctuateAction(s)` (helpers.js, beside `toFirstPerson`): appends `.` unless the text already ends in `.`/`!`/`?`/`…` (optionally followed by a closing quote/paren); idempotent, so it's safe to re-run on stored data every reload. | 89 | **On a sleep, the clock should roll forward to the start of the next Day.** TLDR: the campaign clock (#73) is accurate but a night's sleep just adds its stated duration (e.g. `[TIME_ADVANCE:8h]`), so mornings land at odd offsets — the user wants a sleep to advance "however many minutes it takes to start the next Day" instead. Engine-side arithmetic per the #73 charter (the GM does ZERO time math): compute `1440 − (clockNow() % 1440)` and advance — via the Rest path (`restSpells`, game.js) and/or a sleep-marked advance (e.g. `[TIME_ADVANCE:sleep]` resolving engine-side; one tag_table entry). ✅ Fork ① RATIFIED (user + recommendation converged 2026-07-23): **the Day boundary IS dawn — `clock % 1440 == 0` ≡ dawn, nominally 6am** — so "roll to next Day start" and "wake at dawn" are the same operation (next multiple of 1440; no offset constant, no migration — Day numbers don't move, the boundary just gains an interpretation). Consequence accepted with eyes open: Days are dawn-to-dawn adventuring days, and the future C5 calendar inherits that (fine — day-counted deadlines are boundary-offset-invariant; C4's eventual time-of-day mapping = `(clock % 1440)/60 + 6`). ✅ Fork ② RATIFIED (user 2026-07-23): GM-narrated sleeps too — a sleep-marked advance (`[TIME_ADVANCE:sleep]`, one tag_table entry, engine-resolved) alongside the Rest button. ✅ Fork ③ RATIFIED (user 2026-07-23): overnight sleep ONLY — and the distinguisher IS the marker: the GM is instructed to emit `sleep` solely for a full overnight rest (naps/short rests keep duration advances like `[TIME_ADVANCE:1h]`), and the Rest button is an overnight rest by definition, so no heuristic ever guesses. Jump-safety (C3 threshold firing) already handles schedules crossed by the roll. ⚠ Clock write path = drift surface (tag_table/clock.js/buildClockBlock) — Fable-tier; the #73 subsystem is also still pending its Fable review (todo_checkWithFable #3), so this could ride that pass. | S | Fable | ✅ **Done (v1.433, 2026-07-23, built by Fable).** Better shape found during the build: the GM already HAD the overnight marker — `[REST:long]`, doc'd as "a night's sleep" — so NO new tag; the roll (`clockSleepRoll`, clock.js: next multiple of 1440, at-boundary sleeps a full day) lives in ONE site, `restSpells()`, which both the Rest button and the `[REST:long]` handler call — double-roll impossible by construction. Same-response `[TIME_ADVANCE:]` tags are ABSORBED loudly when `[REST:long]` is present (the 28h-overshoot guard; an under-advance is bounded and self-correcting, an over-advance is not). Bonus fixes riding along: the 30d per-response advance cap with loud clamp (the #73 review's flagged silent-failure), and the Rest button now works for spell-less characters (the old early return made a Warrior's Rest a total no-op). Doc lines updated (TIME_ADVANCE exception + REST dawn contract + `[TIME:dawn]` glue), clock block states dawn-to-dawn days, golden doc hash re-baselined deliberately (+369 chars). 6 new engine tests incl. the C3 composition (a rest jumping a deadline still fires it) and the legit-21d-skip-not-clamped case; 805 green. |

| 27 | **PRE-COMMIT SELF-SABOTAGE — the hygiene suite's fixture repos hijacked the MAIN repo via inherited git env (2026-08-14 incident).** TLDR: committing from an environment that exports repo-location git vars (git exports them into hook environments) made tests-todo-hygiene's fixture `git -C <temp> init/add` operate on the REAL repo instead — `core.worktree` got pointed at a temp fixture dir that was deleted minutes later (bricking every worktree-needing git command with "Invalid path"), and fixture stubs (TODO.md="baseline", BUGS.md="bugs", a deliberately corrupt schedule.json) were STAGED over the real trackers; the same-day #26 hardening commit was blocked mid-flight by its own gate. Repaired by hand: `core.worktree` removed from .git/config, the five poisoned index paths restored to HEAD — no fixture commits landed and the working tree was never touched. Mechanism reproduced in isolation BEFORE the fix (plain GIT_DIR reproduces the index poisoning; GIT_DIR+GIT_WORK_TREE reproduces the core.worktree write). Fix: the fixture `git()` helper scrubs every repo-location env var (GIT_DIR, GIT_WORK_TREE, GIT_INDEX_FILE, GIT_COMMON_DIR, GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY, GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES, GIT_PREFIX) so fixtures resolve their repo from `-C` alone; red-first regression test (a decoy repo must survive fixture git under hostile env), sabotage-proven; `dev/doc-size.js` spawns git with the same scrub from birth. Residual: other dev/ tools that spawn git against fixtures or clones (sabotage.js's scratch clone, session-check's own read-only git) not yet audited for the same class — routed to the #18 census continuation as a verifier-trust exhibit. **SECOND INSTANCE proved the residual real the next day (2026-08-15):** a PATHSPEC commit's pre-commit hook (git exports absolute GIT_DIR + temp GIT_INDEX_FILE for those) deterministically killed `dev/tests-file-forensics.js` — its fixture `git()` has no scrub — while plain commits and direct runs stayed green (why the gate had seemed healthy all day). Fixed at the ONE chokepoint: `dev/run-standalone-suites.js` now spawns every suite with the repo-location vars scrubbed (children inherit the clean env), red→green proven under the poisoned env. Per-suite fixture scrubs beyond tests-todo-hygiene's, and a sabotage pin for the runner scrub, stay routed to the census. **THIRD INSTANCE (caught 2026-08-16, owner: 'bad data is bad data'): IDENTITY leakage** — during the two failed 2026-08-15 gate runs, tests-file-forensics' fixture 'git config user.name' executed under the leaked hook GIT_DIR and wrote 'Forensics Fixture <forensics@example.invalid>' into the MAIN repo config; **39 pushed commits over two days (both sessions) are misattributed**. Repairs: real identity restored; forensics git() now scrubs the 7 repo-location vars (the cure); the pre-commit gained an IDENTITY TRIPWIRE (fixture-looking user.name/email blocks the commit loudly — catches ANY future leak at commit 1, not commit 39); .mailmap maps the fake identity to pmegow so tooling displays correct attribution. | 3 | Portrait storage bloat / localStorage quota — portraits (base64, ~22–52KB each) dominate state size and are multiplied several ways: (a) **companion portraits stored 2×** — both `npc.portrait` and `npc.charSheet.portrait` hold the same image; (b) **`snapshotActiveCamp()` duplicates the entire state** (incl. all portraits) into `tnd_camp_<id>_*` on EVERY `saveAll`, so the active campaign is stored twice (~884KB doubled in the Ammut save). With multiple campaigns this multiplies → real mobile-quota risk (iOS Safari is strict). When quota is hit, `saveCore` catches it, toasts "storage full", and falls back to in-memory `_m` — so the save silently doesn't persist across reload. **Fixes:** dedupe companion portrait (single source); skip the redundant snapshot of the *active* campaign (only snapshot on switch-away — `tnd_core_v10` already is the active state); consider storing portraits in their own dedup'd localStorage keys so snapshots don't copy them. All display reads go through `npcPortrait()` (helpers.js, charSheet-first with npc fallback for pre-migration saves); writes single-home (portrait modal, swap demotion, all 3 companion-creation points, both server restore paths); `migrateWorldState` moves a lone `npc.portrait` into the sheet and drops the duplicate. Measured on t198: 49KB off worldState, all 3 companions display intact. v1.62 half (snapshot removal) unchanged. **⚠ v1.227 — the REAL quota killer found + fixed: the transcript, not portraits.** A t308 Runelords save (Ammut) hit "storage full" on mobile; byte-budget of the export: `worldState.transcript` = **417KB / 54%** of a 772KB blob (617 append-only entries), portraits already deduped (`npc.portrait`=0, only 4 legit portraits = 145KB).
